## Consequential mathematical corrections

- Scan 009, Second upper formula: lower bracket labelled p23: The bracket identifies the composite p12 rho, not p12 rho delta1, with p23.
- Scan 009, Third upper formula: lower bracket labelled p31: The bracket identifies p12 rho squared with p31.
- Scan 009, Annotation below delta0 p0(x) in the right-hand product: Preserves the vertical relation between the bracketed factor and 1.
- Scan 009, Clause beginning qui ne rencontre pas: The restriction to orbits not meeting delta_1(E_1) remains in the live sentence.
- Scan 009, Full central diagram: Restores the objects, labels, and visible incidence of the diagram; the lower long stroke is not attached to F cubed.
- Scan 009, Tuple product beneath the mapping: The tiny base under the product is unresolved. No mathematical repair of the displayed tuple has been made.
- Scan 009, Before the second x-like letter in the tuple formula: Preserves the unmatched closing parenthesis rather than completing the tuple.
- Scan 011, Entire bottom Hyp 1′ passage: The cancelled proposed condition is distinguished from the live Hyp 1.
- Scan 011, Beginning of second line of the cancelled block, before A/α₀: Restores the written description of the slice functor as induced.
- Scan 011, Slanted lower-left margin: The author states why the proposed Hyp 1′ is redundant.
- Scan 012, Slice-category product after indexées: Removes H’s supplied final multiplication cross; the written expression lacks that separator.
- Scan 012, Second arrow label in the lower diagram: Restores the written object on which the identity acts.
- Scan 012, First indexing variable in the parenthetical gloss: The first map is indexed by v, matching the first arrow label.
- Scan 012, Second indexing variable in the parenthetical gloss: The second map is indexed by u, matching the second arrow label.
- Scan 012, Insertion above the final cancelled word: The written replacement is a plus, not an implication arrow.
- Scan 013, Slice-functor arrow in Hyp. 2′: The source may carry a label or an alteration; the reading does not certify an epimorphism-like arrow.
- Scan 013, Target of the slice functor: Preserves the written denominator α₀; the expected image-object notation U_{α₀} is discussed only in the notes.
- Scan 015, Last word of the page: Retains the written question about left exactness; no additional Quand question is supplied.
- Scan 017, retraction letter, all top and dual occurrences: Retraction is named K, including K*.
- Scan 017, finite-set E in the top definitions, diagrams, and lower nondual maps: Keeps the visibly distinguished finite-set alphabet.
- Scan 017, projector label, M0784: Separates the projector label p from the later cyclic permutation rho.
- Scan 017, upper second relation range, M0786: Preserves the explicitly written bound n >= 2.
- Scan 017, upper third relation range, M0788: Preserves the bound on the relation involving the group of index n-1.
- Scan 017, transposition delimiters: Does not normalize a transposition delimiter to the expected pair of parentheses.

- Cancelled short item after correspond: `à` — Inherited C proposal under an opaque cancellation; no complete letter can be independently confirmed.
- Small mark following F=varinjlim alpha: `\ldots` — Point–wavering stroke–point is visible. An ellipsis with an elongated central mark is possible; it remains unresolved.
- Separate dark mark before On voit: `.` — The isolated mark is low relative to the neighboring writing and has small spur-like tails. It could be the period supplied by C or a stray ink blot. New doubt only; the period is retained in place with uncertainty.
- Cancelled peut-on avoir at the foot of the page: `peut-on avoir` — The long strike masks connecting letters. H marks this phrase doubtful; the first reading adds the same caution to C rather than claiming complete recovery.

### Still unresolved

- all inclusion/primed inclusion symbols and their duals: `\delta` — Every local occurrence has a curved upper-left entry into a closed lower loop. C uses delta and H partial; no decisive contrasting form settles this family. Delta is ranked first without excluding partial. This newly added doubt is not a settled correction.
- all group glyphs, supplied marks M0781–M0801 where applicable: `\mathcal G` — Consistent enclosing rounded stroke, interior bar/loop, and upper hook look G-like. The local finite permutation context cannot by itself identify a letter. Ranked uncertainty only; no counted correction.
- i-prime equality, M0789: `déf` — Tiny below-equality annotation is less complete than the legible déf of the projection formula.
- alpha-chain second arrow, M0798: `n-2` — Flattened last subscript digit does not securely distinguish 2 from 1.
- rho inverse note abbreviation, M0799: `élément.` — Tall initial and overlapping strokes leave the abbreviated word unresolved. No completion to élémentaire is made.
- lower primed map name, before the corrected domain and codomain: `m` — Small initial map subscript has two hump-like strokes but remains compatible with n; the following pointwise formula has n. Written in place as m|n, not a settled correction.
- cancelled alpha-pair subscript: `\deleted{\unsure{p+q}}` — Dense zigzag crossing and overlapping replacement strokes prevent recovery of every underlying letter/digit. The C reading remains explicitly doubtful.
- cancelled beta-pair subscript: `\deleted{\unsure{p+q}}` — Dense zigzag crossing and overlapping replacement strokes prevent recovery of every underlying letter/digit. The C reading remains explicitly doubtful.
- cancelled lower-map domain subscript: `\deleted{\unsure{m+1}}` — Dense zigzag crossing and overlapping replacement strokes prevent recovery of every underlying letter/digit. The C reading remains explicitly doubtful.
- cancelled lower-map codomain subscript: `\deleted{\unsure{m}}` — Dense zigzag crossing and overlapping replacement strokes prevent recovery of every underlying letter/digit. The C reading remains explicitly doubtful.
- projector parenthesis, opening word: `projection` — First and main readers differ on the rapid final connected strokes; the existing p05 crop does not securely separate ion from eur. Both readings are retained in place.

### Still unresolved

- left marginal alpha/F and beta/G connectors: `\alpha-F\quad\beta-G` — The right ends are thickened; the beta/G connector is slightly wedge-shaped, but no distinct pair of head strokes can be securely isolated. C's unheaded reading remains first.
- A/phi/topos connector: `\overset{\varphi}{\rule{1cm}{.4pt}}` — A single small wedge caps the right end. A headed arrow is possible but is not secure against a thickened terminal return.
- upper adjunction label: `v=\varphi_!` — The first letter is heavily overwritten. The descending and rising strokes favour v, but the underlying filled area prevents certainty; no earlier character is inferred.
- lower adjunction label: `u=\varphi^*` — The first letter has a bowl and retraced right stroke, favouring u. Retracing obscures the lower junction, so a local doubt remains; no separate deleted layer is invented.

### Still unresolved

- First sketch, both épi arrowheads: `\begin{tikzcd}[ampersand replacement=\&,column sep=small,row sep=small]
I\arrow[r,"\text{épi}"]\arrow[dr,"\text{épi}"']\&J\arrow[r,no head]\arrow[dr,no head,bend right]\&{}\\
\&K\&{}
\end{tikzcd}` — The heads are dark and retraced; no independent pair of tips is securely separated. H's double-point statement is an editorial note and is not evidence for a supplied second tip.
- Cancelled lower-middle node of I,J,J' grid: `\deleted{\unsure{K'}}` — A tall stroke and diagonal branches survive but the cancellation obscures their complete form and any prime.
- X grid, lower-right letter: `\unsure{X'_2|x'_2}` — This crossing is smaller and more cursive than the other X forms; size alone does not securely decide upper versus lower case.
- X square, right vertical endpoint: `\arrow[u,no head,to path={
([yshift=2pt]\tikztostart.north) --
([yshift=-7pt]\tikztotarget.south)
.. controls ([xshift=3pt,yshift=-3pt]\tikztotarget.south)
and ([xshift=-3pt,yshift=-2pt]\tikztotarget.south)
.. ([xshift=-3pt,yshift=-5pt]\tikztotarget.south)\tikztonodes}]` — The curl is clear but does not form the complete chevron seen at left. Geometry is retained without supplying direction.
- Isolated fork, horizontal label: `\unsure{\delta|\partial}_n` — An upper curved stroke returns into a small lower loop. The local shape admits both letters; no definition on this page resolves them.
- Small struck trace beside isolated fork: `\deleted{\unsure{u}}` — Several long strokes cross the low loop; every letter is not plain.
- Case II, both curved labels: `\unsure{\delta|\partial}_n` — Both labels repeat the hooked/looped form; the full square was read, including the written n-1 slip.
- Case III, top curved label: `\unsure{\delta|\partial}_n` — The top tail and lower loop do not uniquely distinguish delta from partial.
- Case IV, cancelled subscript after n: `\deleted{\unsure{*}}` — Dense retracing covers the complete underlying form.
- Case IV, cancelled right-edge label: `\deleted{\unsure{\alpha}}` — Looped ink is interrupted by the cancellation; a complete alpha cannot be recovered.
- Case IV, cancelled bottom-edge label: `\deleted{\unsure{\alpha}}` — The small loop and return stroke remain crossed and thickened.
- Case I, operator in lower-left subscript: `\mathbb E_{p\unsure{+}q}` — The ink between p and q is a small rounded/horizontal mark, without a secure vertical crossing. No expected coproduct index is supplied.
- First table, deleted numeral after n=1: `\deleted{\unsure{2}}` — Cancellation obscures its middle and lower contours.
- First table, deleted numeral after n=2: `\deleted{\unsure{3}}` — The original two-lobe contour cannot be separated from the filled crossing strokes.
- Marginal cancelled p-like mark: `\deleted{\unsure{p|*}}` — The vertical stem and right loop favour a p-like trace, but its underlying identity remains insecure under the long diagonal.
- Cancelled upper E-to-E sketch, left node: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathbb E'}}` — A detached raised short stroke is visible, but its form and cancellation prevent a prime/1 decision.
- Cancelled upper E-to-E sketch, right node: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathbb E'}}` — Cancellation overlays the space where a raised stroke would stand; none is securely isolated.
- Cancelled E square, lower-left raised stroke: `\deleted{\mathbb E^{\unsure{\prime|1}}}` — A short raised upright survives next to E, but lacks a decisive numeral head or distinct prime slant.
- Cancelled E square, lower-right raised stroke: `\deleted{\mathbb E^{\unsure{\prime|1}}}` — The short raised stroke is crossed at its side by a cancellation loop and cannot securely be assigned prime or 1.
- Cancelled X square, lower-right letter: `\deleted{\unsure{X|x}_1}` — The lower-right crossing is visibly smaller and more cursive than the upper X; case is not forced from diagram meaning.
- Cancelled X square, right vertical connection: `\arrow[u,no head,"{\deleted{1}}"',to path={
([xshift=-3pt,yshift=2pt]\tikztostart.north)
.. controls ([xshift=2pt,yshift=1pt]\tikztostart.north)
and ([xshift=1pt,yshift=5pt]\tikztostart.north)
.. ([yshift=8pt]\tikztostart.north)
-- ([yshift=-7pt]\tikztotarget.south)
.. controls ([xshift=3pt,yshift=-3pt]\tikztotarget.south)
and ([xshift=-3pt,yshift=-2pt]\tikztotarget.south)
.. ([xshift=-3pt,yshift=-5pt]\tikztotarget.south)\tikztonodes}]` — The upper end is a curl and the lower end bends; the left vertical provides a contrasting complete chevron. The right label 1 is clear.
- Case V, bottom connection endpoint: `\begin{tikzcd}[ampersand replacement=\&,column sep=small,row sep=small]
\mathbb E_n\arrow[r,"K_{n-1}"]\arrow[d,"K''_{n-1}"']\&\mathbb E_{n-1}\arrow[d]\\
\mathbb E_{n-1}\arrow[r,no head,to path={(\tikztostart.east) -- ([xshift=-2pt]\tikztotarget.west) -- ++(-4pt,5pt)\tikztonodes}]\&\mathbb E_{n-2}
\end{tikzcd}` — A short upper oblique meets the shaft at the right endpoint. No separate lower branch appears; the visible partial geometry is preserved.

### Still unresolved

- M0807: `\unsure{\mathcal G_2|\mathfrak S_2}` — The enclosing loop, internal bar and upper hook are visible, but they do not securely distinguish calligraphic G from the alternative ornate S.
- M0808: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — The third-index glyph repeats the looped, barred group sign. The index 3 is clear; the alphabet remains open.
- M0809: `\unsure{\mathcal G_2|\mathfrak S_2}` — The sign outside the triangle contour has the same enclosing curve and internal stroke; no independent stroke settles the alphabet.
- M0811: `\unsure{\delta_1|\partial_1|p_1}` — The curved entry and lower loop favour the inclusion-family glyph, but retracing obscures the stem; delta/partial is itself open, and p1 is not excluded by the surviving strokes.
- M0812: `\unsure{2}` — The small index has a curved upper stroke and a broad base consistent with 2, but its filled retracing prevents a fully secure resolution.
- M0813: `\unsure{3|2}` — Both a lobed 3-like return and a flatter 2-like base lie in the retraced subscript; neither layer can be isolated confidently.
- M0814: `\unsure{1}` — The index under K is heavily filled; a narrow upright is plausible but every digit stroke is not separable.
- M0815: `\unsure{\mathcal G_2|\mathfrak S_2}` — The looped group sign remains ambiguous. The tall slender stroke to its left is visibly separate and is excluded from the letterform.
- M0816: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — Enclosing curve, inner bar and upper hook repeat the open group-family form; the index 3 is clear.
- M0817: `\unsure{\mathcal G_2|\mathfrak S_2}` — The prose sign before -ens. retains the same loop/interior-bar form; the context of a group action is not used to choose S.
- M0818: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — The second prose group sign repeats the family; the third index is distinct, while the main glyph remains open.
- M0819: `\unsure{ponctuations|pointations|structures}` — The initial letters overlap the cancelling strokes above pts fixes and the cramped live suffix reaches the right edge. No complete letter sequence separates the alternatives.
- cancelled equation: first glyph: `\unsure{p|\rho}` — The cancellation crosses the head; the looped descender does not distinguish p from rho securely.
- cancelled equation: last glyph: `\unsure{q|\sigma}` — A left oval/right descender favours q, but the struck head does not eliminate sigma.
- inclusion-family labels throughout this page: `\unsure{\delta|\partial}` — The curved entry into a closed lower loop repeats the current unresolved delta/partial family; no page-local definition settles it. Index and arrow geometry decisions are independent of this letter doubt.
- upper circled square: top connector: `blunt-left shaft with rounded right-end return` — The right end curls back left; neither witness direction is imposed. The literal geometry is drawn in place.
- E3 square: right vertical endpoint: `shaft with leftward foot` — Only one small leftward terminal stroke is visible, without an opposite wing.
- lower circled square: left vertical endpoint: `rounded upper cap/loop` — A rounded return covers the upper end; the complete chevron visible on the right vertical is absent here.
- dark horizontal two-point strip: deletion state: `\unsure{\deleted{\figure{...}}}` — The added strokes run along or under the segment rather than independently across it. C deletion state is retained under an explicit local state doubt.
- dual system: lower-left K star index: `K_{\unsure{1}}^*` — The subscript is heavily filled; its upright form is compatible with 1 but not completely exposed.
- cancelled E-like start: `\deleted{$\unsure{\mathbb E}$}` — The double upright is visible; lower-right strokes are obscured. No independent subscript is supplied from context.
- cancelled inclusion-like start: `\deleted{$\unsure{\delta|\partial}_{\unsure{2}}$}` — The letter is crossed; a lower-right bent trace supports but does not settle the small 2.
- cancelled pts fixes: `\deleted{\unsure{pts fixes}}` — The crossing strokes obscure some letters. The agreement of the witnesses does not make the whole phrase clear enough to resolve.

### Still unresolved

- M0820, upper-left pair definition: `\zeta` — Bent upper course and long descender favor zeta, but the simplified cursive form does not firmly exclude xi.
- M0821, argument of d sigma: `\zeta` — The glyph is reduced almost to a long upright with a lower return; it belongs to the same family as the two other occurrences but has little internal distinguishing detail.
- M0822, argument of rho squared d: `\zeta` — One bent upper part and a long tail match the initial glyph. The alternative remains explicit because no decisive contrasting form occurs on this page.
- upper-right triple, last coordinate: `x` — The small glyph has a low arch and short right stem, with no unequivocal crossing like the clearer middle coordinates. Local x forms vary, so x remains first, but n cannot be ruled out by the ink. This is a newly added doubt only, not a settled correction against C.

### Still unresolved

- three group labels and the cancelled opening sign: `\mathcal G` — Rounded enclosing loop, interior bar/loop and upper hook favor a G-like form; no decisive letter contrast permits a global resolution.
- all point-variable occurrences, M0826–M0857 except M0838: `\zeta` — Bent upper courses and long descenders vary with retracing; the family is consistent, but its name remains unresolved. Primes are read as separate features.
- rho squared coordinate output closing delimiter: `]` — The closing mark has an angular upper stroke and leftward foot; it could also be a rapidly squared parenthesis. Both candidates are retained in place.
- bottom p rho d sigma argument closing delimiter: `]` — The same angular closing ductus appears here; the choice is kept consistent with the rho-squared output rather than silently assigning a different delimiter.
- left underbrace, between set and g: `\rotatebox{90}{$\in$}` — A small upward-open form resembles vertical membership but partly meets g’s upper loop.
- extra loop/cross on right box boundary: `\textcircled{*}` — A loop and crossed strokes touch the doubled border. It may be an earlier circled star overlapped by the border; neither a separate letter nor deletion state is asserted.
- cancelled initial group sign: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal G|\mathfrak S}}` — Crossing, retracing or deletion obscures distinguishing strokes. Every underlying character/prime is not plain, so the deleted-text uncertainty is retained.

### Still unresolved

- M0858: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — The open rounded group outline, upper hook and lower return permit S/G; subscript 3 is clear.
- M0859: `\unsure{élémt}` — A short accented initial e and tall l are clear, but the compressed remaining strokes do not securely separate the abbreviated ending.
- M0860: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3|\deleted{\mathcal G}_3|\deleted{\mathfrak S}_3}` — The group outline and 3 are visible. Two diagonal strokes cross the letter core; neither group identity nor deletion versus overwriting is settled.
- M0861: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — The open rounded form has a lower horizontal return and no X overstroke. Its alphabet remains S/G.
- M0862: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3|\deleted{\mathcal G}_3|\deleted{\mathfrak S}_3}` — The complete formula was read. The same rounded group body is crossed by two diagonals; the subscript 3 and wedge with subscript {1,sigma1} are clear.
- M0863: `\unsure{le reste}` — The low connected letters after the star-comma group remain crossed by main-text strokes; le reste is plausible but not fully separated.
- M0864: `\unsure{étant}` — The small word between satisfaire and the following run is intersected by independent main-text ink; not all letters are plain.
- M0865: `\unsure{formel}` — An initial tall f-like stroke and trailing low run survive; the middle and end are insufficiently distinct to settle formel.
- M0866: `\unsure{par ces trois formules}` — The initial p and later f-like stem are visible, but intervening short words and joined strokes remain interrupted by other ink.
- M0867: `\unsure{cas|(a)}` — An open left curve, compact central a-like form and long right curve allow either letters cas or parenthesised a. No final full stop is visible.
- M0868: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — The full defining formula and annotation were read: X3 star = X3 minus X3 prime, with group dot d(X2) below. The group alphabet remains S/G.
- M0869: `\unsure{\mathcal G_3|\mathfrak S_3}` — The group has its open round body and lower return, without the X overstroke seen earlier. S/G is still not distinguished.
- M0870: `\unsure{\zeta|\xi}` — The full existence line is legible. Its first Greek letter is a tall kinked curve, compatible with zeta or a compressed xi.
- M0871: `\unsure{\zeta|\xi}` — The full pair of equations is legible. The first right-hand side has a short upper hook and several lower bends, without a decisive zeta/xi distinction.
- right marginal first triple, third argument: `\unsure{z|1}` — The first third-argument glyph is a narrow upright with a short upper bend and no clear lower foot. Nearby third-argument forms have curved tops and longer lower returns. z remains first but the local z/1 form is not settled; this adds uncertainty only.

- struck word before au moins: `\deleted{\unsure{deux|donc}}` — The d-led word and possible final cross survive only partly; the middle letters cannot settle deux versus donc.
- NB: first Sigma subscript: `\Sigma_{\unsure{1|0}}` — p05: the index is a broad, nearly filled oval/short upright after Sigma. Retracing obscures the interior; a secure contrast between 1 and 0 cannot be isolated. This is a new local doubt, not a settled correction.
- case a): handwritten empty-set sign: `\unsure{\phi|\varnothing}` — p09: a small closed loop is crossed by a long oblique stem extending much farther below than above the loop. That ductus favors phi, but a handwritten slashed empty-set oval can share it and there is no same-page contrasting phi. The empty-set meaning does not settle the glyph name. This is uncertainty-only, not a settled correction count.

- opening cancelled words: `\deleted{\unsure{donc}};\quad\deleted{\unsure{que}}` — Crossing strokes cover the interiors; neither word is resolved.
- inclusion-letter family: `\unsure{\delta|\partial}` — Curved entry and lower loop repeat the current delta/partial family; indices are read independently.
- first-square connector ends: `rounded/footed shafts without completed heads` — The four ends are drawn by their visible geometry; conventional arrow directions are not inferred from the square.
- cancelled K-like label: `\deleted{\unsure{K_0|K}}` — A K-like upright/arms and lower-right loop survive, but the loop may be part of the crossing strokes.
- crossed sign after first coordinate identity: `\deleted{\unsure{=}}` — The small black group is crossed and its two-bar reading remains tentative.
- crossed glyph before rho-squared: `\deleted{\unsure{\rho}}` — The long strike leaves only an upright/curved remnant.
- first crossed exponent2: `\rho^{\deleted{\unsure{2}}}` — The compact exponent is heavily overwritten. H already explicitly notes its cancellation; a state wrapper alone is not an H correction.
- cancelled et quand: `\deleted{\unsure{et quand}}` — Only portions of tall and descending strokes survive the horizontal strike.
- second crossed exponent2: `\rho^{\deleted{\unsure{2}}}` — The same tiny exponent is crossed in the point-pair formula; no resolution from the needed cyclic power.
- cancelled alors: `\deleted{\unsure{alors}}` — Baseline letters lie under the long strike and the descending annotation stroke.
- cancelled equality plus ce: `\deleted{\unsure{\zeta|\xi}=\unsure{\zeta\prime|\xi\prime}=e_2\quad\unsure{ce}}` — The equality is crossed; the trailing short word is not fully exposed.
- short word before diagram label: `\unsure{du|de}` — A tall d-like stem and low rounded continuation form a separate word, but u/e is not separable.
- crossed preimage subscript: `\deleted{\unsure{12}}` — The dense subscript block cannot be fully separated into digits.
- Mais line main formulas: `\unsure{p_{31}(x,y,z)}=\unsure{(z,x)}=\unsure{p\rho(x,y,z)|p\rho^2(x,y,z)};\quad\unsure{i.e.};\quad\unsure{p_{31}=p\rho|p_{31}=p\rho^2}` — After the necessary enlarged-scope crop the multiple crossing layers still obscure letters, arguments and raised exponent positions. Alternatives are retained, not solved from the desired projection equation.
- lowered tuple in Mais line: `\deleted{\inserted{\unsure{(z,x,y)}}}` — A distinct lower pair of parentheses and intervening tuple-like strokes are visible, but not every z/x/y stroke is secure.
- tiny cancelled start before second preimage: `\deleted{\unsure{puis}}` — The short crossed word precedes live i.e.; its internal sequence remains unreadable enough to retain the C doubt.
- cancelled baseline after group definition: `\deleted{\unsure{alors un objet de}\quad\unsure{X_3}}` — The baseline is struck and its final symbol is overwritten; the clearer upper insertion does not resolve the lower layer.
- cancelled X3-prime s’écrit: `\deleted{X_3\prime\quad\unsure{s’écrit}}` — The upper letter portions survive but the long strike hides several joins.
- tiny fragment before prouvons: `\deleted{\unsure{a|à}}` — A small counter and crossing oblique stroke suggest a/à only tentatively; no accent or complete word is forced.
- cancelled fragment before si/2°: `\deleted{\unsure{donc}}` — Crossing diagonals and a lower loop can look like an indexed symbol, but neither it nor the C word candidate is secure.
- live si/2°: `\unsure{si|$2^\circ$}` — The curled first shape and raised small dark point can be read as si or an ordinal marker; source phrasing is not used to force one.
- bottom cancelled formula: `\deleted{$\unsure{(p\sigma_3d)(x,y)}=\unsure{p\rho d(x,y)}=\unsure{\mathrm{id}}$}` — Dense superimposed cancellation prevents resolution of all factor letters, indices and coordinates; the surviving terminal id-like shape alone does not resolve the whole formula.
- terminal word case: `\unsure{Qed|qed}` — The looped initial and long descending stroke do not securely distinguish the capital/lowercase form; the absent final stop is independent.

- M0920: `\unsure{simple|souple}` — The initial s is followed by a short low mark and a long joined middle run, then a p descent and tall looped l. The middle i/m versus o/u sequence is not securely separated; no decision is made from the phrase’s expected sense.

- germe d’espace(s): `\unsure{d'espaces|d'espace}` — The last connected run ends in a downward return. A separate final s cannot be distinguished securely from that return, so neither witness’s number is imposed.

- M0921: `\unsure{l'inclusion}` — The long run after c’est que has l/inclusion-like strokes, but overlapping main-text ink prevents every letter from being separated. The visible elevated pair after the run is recorded independently of the lexical doubt.
- marginal quotation placement: `\unsure{l'inclusion}'' … équivalence d'homotopie''` — The two visible elevated pairs are retained at their positions. Matching openers cannot be isolated securely; the current proposal leaves the pairs unmatched rather than supplying punctuation for symmetry.
- marginal map terminal: `\begin{tikzcd}[ampersand replacement=\&,column sep=small,cramped]
Y\arrow[r,-,to path={(\tikztostart)--(\tikztotarget) node[circle,fill=black,inner sep=.45pt] {} \tikztonodes}]\&\mathcal V_{Y,X}

- 151-028-A02: `\deleted{\unsure{$Z^*$ est|et que}}` — Crossing raised strokes over the first group and the heavy horizontal cancellation obscure the letter sequence. Neither Z-star est nor et que is plain in full.
- reconstituer, initial e: `\unsure{réconstituer|reconstituer}` — Tiny broken dark strokes lie directly above the initial e. An accent is plausible, but a pen trace cannot be excluded. The written accent is not normalised from the standard spelling; this adds doubt only.

### Still unresolved

- opening: mark after amène: `\unsure{à|:}` — p01: an upper oblique short stroke and lower compact dark body favor an accented à, but the reduced body also admits a colon-like pair. No missing letter is supplied merely because the sentence would require à. This is new uncertainty only, with the witnesses’ à retained first.
- terminal stroke after et le diagramme: `\unsure{\deleted{un}|---}` — The terminal stroke has a short initial rise and low humps but no recoverable complete un. A deletion versus a long dash remains open; neither state is silently imposed.
- cancelled prefix before live espace: `\deleted{\unsure{espace|en}}` — The overstrike crosses the word; its initial small loop and p-like descender favor espace, but the remaining letters cannot be established individually. Definite cancellation, unresolved text.
- two dots after du diagramme: `\unsure{:}` — p08: two small, black, diagonally aligned dots plausibly form a colon, but their separation from the last word leaves a possible non-textual-speck interpretation. They are not silently discarded or asserted certain.

### Still unresolved

- opening verb after qu'on: `choisisse` — The ending is a low connected course without a securely isolated tall crossed t. It favors the longer H form, but cannot be read letter by letter. This is newly marked uncertainty, not a settled replacement of C.
- small raised mark after pt base: `\wedge` — Two legs/courses are visible above the separate curved attachment; a complete four-arm x is not secure. Later path notation is not used to supply a base-point letter.
- cancelled et un point: `et un point` — The strike hides some small joins; retain the C candidate under doubt.
- cancelled relatif: `relatif` — Not every letter remains plain under the horizontal strike.
- et before il resterait: `\deleted{et}` — Crossbar and putative cancellation overlap; deletion state remains unresolved while the word is retained.
- curve below the rising -rait and before encore: `\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(word.base),x=1cm,y=1cm]
\node[anchor=base west,inner sep=0pt] (word) at (0,0) {resterait};
\draw[line width=.35pt] (1.03,-.14) .. controls (1.00,.12) and (1.60,.24) .. (1.72,.52);
\end{tikzpicture}` — Its physical course is clear, but it does not identify a replacement or destination securely. The curve is drawn without declaring the ending inserted or deleted.
- condensed last word in first marginal line: `moy\textsuperscript{t}` — The terminal upright is compatible with raised t, but the contracted middle does not expose the full o/y sequence; M0930 remains open. H mod. is recorded in coverage, not silently adopted.
- article in marginal note: `d'un choix` — No unambiguous separate apostrophe survives between d and the joined low strokes; a favored first candidate is insufficient to resolve M0931.
- cancelled target glyph before live Y: `X` — The overstrike is dense and does not leave an independently complete original glyph.

### Still unresolved

- M0932: `\unsure{restant}` — The word has an r/e/s-like low opening run, two high t-like forms and compressed intervening strokes. The image is compatible with restant, but the middle letters are not independently separated well enough to remove C’s doubt. Comme after it is readable.
- 151-032-A01: `\deleted{\unsure{)|?}}` — The long closing lower arc favours a right parenthesis, but the upper zigzag/crossed strokes prevent a secure underlying reading. No detached question dot is visible. The candidate refinement is not a resolution, and the separate following comma remains live.
- 151-032-A02: `\deleted{\unsure{On}}` — A tall rounded opening and low terminal run are crossed repeatedly. The original On candidate remains possible, but not every letter is plain.
- 151-032-A03: `\deleted{\unsure{Id}}` — There is a tall final d-like upright and a compact earlier run under several looping and horizontal strokes. The candidate Id is retained under doubt; the adjacent arrow and Y remain live.
- base-Y map target, final subscript: `\mathcal V_{Y,\unsure{\lambda|X}}` — The final small glyph has a roof-like meeting of oblique descending strokes. No full four-armed crossing like the clear X subscripts elsewhere on this same page can be separated. Lambda is ranked first from that shape, while X remains possible; the expected neighbourhood index does not decide it. This adds local uncertainty and is not a separate correction count.

### Still unresolved

- Struck word after d'homotopie: `\deleted{\unsure{longue}}` — The central joins and end are crossed; not every letter is separately recoverable.
- Cancelled quoted run in the ninth line: `\deleted{``\unsure{pt base}''}` — The quote pairs, p/t and b are visible, but the strike obscures parts of base.
- Cancelled fragment after convenable: `\deleted{\unsure{p}}` — The long descender survives, but the upper bowl/attachment is crossed twice.
- Second numeral of the upper sequence label: `(1\unsure{2|1})` — The second numeral has a hooked/arched top and descending stroke, without a secure lower foot; the expected numbering does not settle it.
- First spatial index in the upper sequence: `\unsure{\deleted{Z}z|z|Z}` — The dark angular shape has retracing through its upper and lower runs. An old Z and replacement z cannot be independently separated; single retraced z/Z readings remain possible.
- Cancelled word after the lower i.e. colon: `\deleted{\unsure{donc}}` — Crossing strokes and a descender from the preceding line obscure the initial and middle letters.
- Right-edge ending after conne: `conne\unsure{x}` — The crossed/descending cluster reaches the edge. There is no independently visible contour supporting an additional final e.
- Cancelled final word: `\deleted{\unsure{et}}` — A tall t-like upright survives, but the first low part lies beneath the heavy strike.

### Still unresolved

- cancelled word above que: `cyclique` — The strike obscures central/right joins, so the supplied candidate stays doubtful.
- cancelled old group sign above first diagram object: `\Pi_1` — The dense overstrike leaves only partial roof/leg/index strokes; live Pi forms favor capital Pi without settling the old fragment.
- cancelled old number under first shaft: `(14)` — Several numerical strokes are crossed and cannot be independently separated.
- cancelled word before interviennent: `sont` — The middle joins remain hidden by cancellation.
- initial case of théorie: `théorie` — The unusually tall initial has a mid-height crossbar resembling lower-case t in types/trouve on this sheet. Its case remains uncertain; p12 clips the extreme top, while the already viewed original/half supply full height. No new crop or capitalization by expectation is used.

### Still unresolved

- M0938: `\unsure{ss}` — Two compact looped/upright s-like forms stand above the gap after la. Their exact individual strokes remain compressed; the written candidate ss is retained without expanding it.
- M0939: `\unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F}` — The first ornate sign has a looped cap, multiple crossed bars and a curled lower return. These do not securely distinguish the calligraphic and Fraktur alphabet alternatives. The separate right-hand stroke is its insertion leader.
- M0940: `\unsure{\mathcal F|\mathfrak F}` — The second sign repeats the same looped/barred family. Its lower curve and upper loop remain compatible with both supplied alphabets. The independent curved leader is followed separately.
- M0941: `\unsure{\leftrightarrow|\Leftrightarrow}` — Two opposed tips are visible, excluding the ordinary word en as the source shape. The heavily retraced body leaves single versus double shaft uncertain. The alternatives are narrowed to leftrightarrow/Leftrightarrow without declaring a strike or resolving the supplied mark.
- 151-035-A01: `\deleted{\unsure{Z}}` — The low horizontal foot and a diagonal support Z, but several upper zigzags overlap its top structure. The existing struck-text doubt is retained.
- 151-035-A02: `\deleted{\unsure{formés}}` — The f is visible, but a dense horizontal strike covers much of the following low run. Not every letter is plain.
- 151-035-A03: `\deleted{\unsure{d'un}}` — The initial d and apostrophe remain visible. The following letters are obscured by the strong horizontal cancellation, so the un ending receives local doubt.
- terminal after triviaux in16: `\unsure{,|;}` — A heavy retraced terminal has a lower oblique/hooked course and upper dark strokes. Comma versus semicolon cannot be separated confidently; no extra certainty-only correction is counted.
- first raised short étale form: `\unsure{étle|ét.le}` — Accented e, t, l and final e are visible, without a separate a. The point between t and l is not securely isolated. Both alternatives preserve the written short form.
- X superscript in first covering parenthesis: `X^{\unsure{*|+}}` — The small raised glyph has crossing strokes and a heavy lower-left return, but fewer separate arms than the clear base-line stars. Asterisk/plus remains local doubt.
- last neighbourhood final index at paper edge: `\Pi_1\mathcal V^*_{Y,\unsure{\lambda|X}}` — The cramped final index has roof-like oblique strokes near the paper edge. A complete four-armed crossing is not secure. Lambda is ranked from the visible shape while X remains possible, without repairing from the expected notation.
